path = \"<file>\"\n   boundary = uuid.uuid4().hex\n   def part_field(name, value):\n       return (f\"--{boundary}\\r\\nContent-Disposition: form-data; name=\\\"{name}\\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n{value}\\r\\n\").encode()\n   def part_file(name, filename, content):\n       return (f\"--{boundary}\\r\\nContent-Disposition: form-data; name=\\\"{name}\\\"; filename=\\\"{filename}\\\"\\r\\n\"\n               f\"Content-Type: text/markdown\\r\\n\\r\\n\").encode() + content + b\"\\r\\n\"\n   body  = part_field(\"chat_id\", chat_id)\n   body += part_field(\"caption\", \"<short caption>\")\n   body += part_file(\"document\", path.split('/')[-1], open(path, \"rb\").read())\n   body += f\"--{boundary}--\\r\\n\".encode()\n   req = urllib.request.Request(f\"https://api.telegram.org/bot{tok}/sendDocument\",\n       data=body, headers={\"Content-Type\": f\"multipart/form-data; This session discovered WHY the\n25|manual step is load-bearing and automated it:\n26|\n27|- Clones of a template share the parent's **machine-id** → systemd-networkd derives the DHCP\n28|  client DUID from machine-id → **all clones present the SAME DHCP client-id**.\n29|- Live-proven: clones 132 and 133 both leased **10.0.20.225** with identical DUID\n30|  (`ff7409e91300020000ab115d2215018a7fe920`) — the OPNsense Kea lease table shows one lease row;\n31|  the second clone just squats (duplicate-address impostor class at DHCP level).\n32|- SSH host keys are also cloned → identical host key fingerprints across clones.\n33|\n34|### Fix (inside template 135)\n35|\n36|`/etc/systemd/system/acms-clone-reidentify.service` (oneshot, Before=network.target+ssh.service,\n37|enabled) runs `/usr/local/sbin/acms-clone-reidentify.sh`:\n38|\n39|1. Reads reference id `/etc/acms-template-identity` (the template's baked machine-id).\n40|2. If current machine-id == baked id → `rm /etc/machine-id` (444 perms removed first),\n41|   `systemd-machine-id-setup` (derives from the per-VM UUID → unique per clone), copy to\n42|   `/var/lib/dbus/machine-id`, regenerate ALL SSH host keys, write NEW id back to the reference\n43|   file, `systemctl restart systemd-networkd` → fresh DUID → unique DHCP lease.\n44|3. If ids differ → exit 0 (idempotent; second boot is a no-op).\n45|\n46|## Clone acceptance proof (VM 136 `acms-tpltest-final`, full clone of 135)\n47|\n48|First boot verified live:\n49|\n50|| Check | Result |\n51||---|---|\n52|| machine-id | `abd1e87f…` ≠ template baked `951eec8f…` PASS |\n53|| ed25519 host key | NEW fingerprint `lnvz3p8+6OA6…` (≠ template's `m0B0ZbjG…`) PASS |\n54|| re-identify unit | active(exited), ExecStart status=0 PASS |\n55|| DHCP | unique lease `.227` w/ own MAC `bc:24:11:6c:b1:2f` (Kea table verified) PASS |\n56|| static .203 | absent from netplan PASS |\n57|| aiohttp | 3.14.3 present PASS |\n58|| Hermes | v0.19.0 in /opt/hermes-venv PASS |\n59|| bridge unit | present, inactive (awaits clone-time env) PASS |\n60|| secrets | none inherited (`CLONE_TIME_OVERWRITE` placeholders only, count=2) PASS |\n61|| st-agentd | disabled PASS |\n62|| QGA | active PASS |\n63|\n64|Earlier clones 132/133 (pre-reidentify template) proved the failure mode and were used to develop\n65|the fix; 134/136 (post-fix clones) — 136 fully verified, then **all test clones destroyed**\n66|(132/133/134/136 gone; no leftover IP claims — Kea lease table clean of dead test MACs after\n67|destroy, verified `.223/.224/.225/.226/.227` rows only reference live/test MACs).\n68|\n69|## Clone-time contract (what the provisioning flow must still inject per clone — §4)\n70|\n71|- `/etc/llm-manager-agent.env.TEMPLATE` → copy to `/etc/llm-manager-agent.env` with unique\n72|  `LLM_MANAGER_AGENT_KEY` + `API_SERVER_KEY` (per-worker LLM Manager virtual key)\n73|- `/etc/acms-worker-meta.TEMPLATE` → fill worker metadata (HOSTNAME, WORKER_UID, ACMS_AGENT_UUID,\n74|  VMID, HERMES_PROFILE)\n75|- rename Hermes profile dir `/home/hermes/.hermes/profiles/acms-worker` → per-worker profile name\n76|  (or symlink) matching the unit ExecStart\n77|- write profile `config.yaml` (LLM Manager provider, model policy, caps)\n78|- `systemctl enable --now hermes-bridge`\n79|- ACMS registration (POST /api/v1/agents/register) + ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON append\n80|\n81|## Traps hit (documented for the skill)\n82|\n83|1. **VMID 130 = CT130 CI-runner collision** — `qm clone` failed 3× with \"config file already\n84|   exists\" despite VMID looking free on miam00111; CT130 lives on miam-00133. ALWAYS check\n85|   `/api2/json/cluster/nextid` + cluster resources, never assume local-only.\n86|2. **Templates cannot be booted** (`you can't start a vm if it's a template`) — template content\n87|   must be repaired in a WORKING COPY first, then `qm template` converted.\n88|3. **machine-id is 444 read-only after first-boot** — `systemd-machine-id-setup` silently no-ops\n89|   on an existing (even read-only) file; must `chmod 644` + `rm` first. Symptom: re-identify log\n90|   fires but machine-id unchanged.\n91|4. **DUID follows machine-id** — regenerating machine-id is NOT enough until networkd restarts;\n92|   include `systemctl restart systemd-networkd` in the re-identify path.\n93|5. **`qm clone` outputs \"config file already exists\" but still completes the clone** (rc=0 line\n94|   can follow a partial-create race from a prior timed-out attempt) — always re-verify with\n95|   `qm config <id>` before retrying.\n96|6. **noderun.run() takes script TEXT, not a path** (CLI form takes a path).\n97|7. # STEA-004 / ACMS Next Feature Implementation Plan
## Backend Hygiene -> Durable IDs / Artifacts / Projects / Cost / Fleet -> Operator UI

**Date:** 2026-10-01  
**Executor:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur or successor implementation agent  
**Systems:** ACMS CT122, LLM Manager / Server Manager VM114, Agent Bridge / Hermes worker fleet, Jira, Service Registry / Homarr  
**Plan type:** next production feature cycle after successful ACMS -> A2A -> Hermes -> LLM Manager acceptance

---

# 0. BLUF

The production execution path works.

Do **not** rebuild it.

Current verified foundation:

```text
Jira -> ACMS reconciliation                    WORKING
ACMS -> A2A dispatch                           WORKING
A2A ACK / one-task lock / idempotency         WORKING
Hermes execution                              WORKING
Qwen3.8-Flash-Next local inference            WORKING
Heartbeat fleet 5/5                           WORKING
Live event/SSE backend                        WORKING
Artifact backend                              WORKING
Products / Projects backend                   WORKING
Inbox backend                                 WORKING
Model-policy precedence                       WORKING
Emporia facility collector backend            WORKING
```

This phase is primarily:

```text
1. Fix backend hygiene first.
2. Fix human IDs, handoffs, Jira-template fidelity, Projects/Products, costs, artifacts, Fleet.
3. Make ACMS operator UI useful.
```

Do functionality first.

Do not spend the implementation window on visual decoration.

---

# 1. User-Priority Execution Order

Execute in this order.

## Phase A - Backend hygiene FIRST

```text
A1. VM123 safe deletion
A2. golden-template 121 repair
A3. Server Manager placement-policy enforcement
A4. placement / runtime reconciliation tests
```

## Phase B - Data / operations features SECOND

```text
B1. Work UIDs
B2. Artifact UIDs
B3. automatic Markdown handoff per completed Work Item
B4. artifact discoverability and specific URLs
B5. Jira-template fidelity
B6. persistent Project pages
B7. Product repository associations
B8. Slop Ratio collection + history
B9. all cost / power visibility in ACMS
B10. Fleet table, sorting, filters, search
B11. global "Search anything"
```

## Phase C - Operator UI THIRD

```text
C1. Home
C2. Work Kanban
C3. Work detail
C4. Agent detail + Hermes chat
C5. Inbox
C6. Products / Projects
C7. Artifacts
C8. Usage / cost
C9. responsive usability
```

---

# 2. Preserve the Proven Production Path

Current production baseline to verify before mutation:

```text
ACMS prod:
  CT122
  SHA f6ac25b
  v0.11.0
  Alembic 0013_agent_uid_naming

LLM Manager:
  VM114
  SHA 66f37a50eaae

Workers:
  acms-hermes-worker-uid-001 -> VM124 -> MIAM00111 -> 10.0.20.203
  acms-hermes-worker-uid-002 -> VM125 -> MIAM00112 -> 10.0.20.206
  acms-hermes-worker-uid-003 -> VM126 -> MIAM00143 -> 10.0.20.207
  acms-hermes-worker-uid-004 -> VM127 -> MIAM00144 -> 10.0.20.208
  acms-hermes-worker-uid-005 -> VM128 -> MIAM00100 -> 10.0.20.209

Worker bridge:
  :8402
```

Run smoke tests before each production release.

Do not regress:

```text
real dispatch
ACK
RUNNING-only-after-ACK
watcher/reconcile completion
live stream
model policy precedence
usage capture
artifact integrity
```

---

# 3. Phase A1 - Delete VM123 Safely

Human authorization is explicit:

```text
VM123 may be deleted.
```

Current problem:

```text
VM123 is stopped duplicate acms-worker-001
and contains static .203 networking.
Starting it risks collision with live VM124.
```

Before destruction:

```text
qm config 123
confirm node
confirm stopped
confirm no unique attached storage
confirm no unique snapshots required
confirm no ACMS runtime record references VM123
confirm no Registry record marks VM123 as active
confirm no backup/rollback requirement remains
```

Capture final config:

```text
raw/VM123-qm-config-before-delete.txt
```

If there is unique disk/state:

```text
STOP
document
do not delete
```

Otherwise destroy VM123 without starting it.

Verify:

```text
VM123 no longer exists
VM124 remains healthy
10.0.20.203 owned only by VM124
uid-001 heartbeat remains fresh
```

---

# 4. Phase A2 - Repair Golden Worker Template 121

The template must stop creating broken clones.

Current known defects:

```text
static 10.0.20.203 baked in
clone identity cleanup required manually
Hermes runtime incomplete
aiohttp missing
bridge bootstrap incomplete
```

Template 121 must contain:

```text
supported base OS
QEMU guest agent
Python/runtime requirements
Hermes Agent
aiohttp
bridge bootstrap code
systemd bridge template/unit support
cloud-init support
no worker-specific API keys
no worker-specific LLM Manager keys
no static worker IP
no copied SSH host keys
no copied machine-id
no copied Agent UID
```

Clone-time data must include:

```text
hostname
worker UID
ACMS agent UUID association
VMID
network configuration
IP / DHCP reservation
Hermes profile name
unique bridge API key
unique LLM Manager agent key
model policy
```

Never create a clone that boots on the network with another worker's IP.

Preferred flow:

```text
clone powered off
-> assign VMID/node/storage
-> inject unique cloud-init/network configuration
-> regenerate machine identity
-> regenerate SSH host keys
-> inject non-secret worker metadata
-> provision secrets securely
-> first boot
-> health check
-> bridge registration
-> ACMS heartbeat
```

Acceptance:

```text
unique machine-id
unique host keys
no .203
aiohttp present
Hermes launches
bridge launches
no secrets inherited
QGA works
network works
```

Use one disposable test clone, then destroy it.

Do not create worker-006 unless separately authorized.

---

# 5. Phase A3 - Enforce Server Manager Placement Policy

Placement must become code, not convention.

General workers:

```text
MIAM00100 preferred
```

Inference workers:

```text
maximum 1 worker per inference node
```

Known inference nodes:

```text
MIAM00111
MIAM00112
MIAM00143
MIAM00144
```

Previously approved critical exclusions:

```text
MIAM00133
MIAM00147
```

Hard filters:

```text
node healthy
not excluded
storage available
RAM above threshold
CPU below threshold
not already at worker cap
```

Preference:

```text
MIAM00100 general-worker bonus
non-inference node preferred after MIAM00100
inference node large penalty
inference node rejected if it already has one worker
```

Human Proxmox migration remains valid.

After a human migrates a worker:

```text
durable Agent UID unchanged
ACMS UUID unchanged
runtime node/VM/IP reconciled
```

---

# 6. Human-Readable Work Item UID

Add immutable human UID:

```text
ACMS-WORK-######-YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS
```

Example:

```text
ACMS-WORK-000127-20261001_082241
```

Rules:

```text
###### = global monotonic 6-digit work sequence
timestamp = Work Item created_at
timezone for UID = UTC
UID immutable after creation
internal UUID remains immutable database identity
```

Use a concurrency-safe DB sequence/allocation mechanism.

Do not derive by `COUNT(*)`.

Backfill existing Work Items by:

```text
created_at ASC
then internal UUID ASC
```

Never reuse a sequence.

Human UI uses Work UID as primary link text.

Internal UUID goes under Technical Details.

---

# 7. Human-Readable Artifact UID

Add immutable human UID:

```text
ACMS-ARTIFACT-######-YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS
```

Example:

```text
ACMS-ARTIFACT-000042-20261001_082612
```

Rules match Work UID:

```text
global monotonic sequence
created_at UTC timestamp
internal UUID preserved
sha256 preserved
```

Backfill deterministically.

---

# 8. Artifact Discoverability Must Be Fixed

Current failure:

Jira referenced:

```text
artifact 699ecf76
sha256 81325074b6ed...
https://10.0.20.122/ui/artifacts
```

but the short ID/hash was not directly findable.

Required specific route:

```text
/ui/artifacts/<artifact_uid>
```

Example:

```text
/ui/artifacts/ACMS-ARTIFACT-000042-20261001_082612
```

Support internal UUID as compatibility alias.

Artifact detail page shows:

```text
Artifact UID
title
type
Jira key
Work UID
Project
Product
Agent
created time
size
sha256 full
sha256 short
BLUF
rendered Markdown
raw/download button
```

Artifact search must match:

```text
Artifact UID
internal UUID
old short ID
full sha256
sha prefix
title
Jira key
Work UID
Project
Product
Agent
```

Jira handoff links must point to the specific artifact, never only the list page.

---

# 9. One Markdown Handoff Per Completed Work Item

Inbox is not the handoff.

For every terminal Work Item, create/finalize one canonical Markdown handoff:

```text
artifact_type = work_handoff
```

For successful work, it is mandatory.

If agent provided a handoff:

```text
use agent handoff as canonical content
```

If no handoff exists:

```text
ACMS generates a concise fallback handoff from durable events
```

Minimum structure:

```markdown
# Work Handoff - <Work UID>

## BLUF

## Requested Work

## What Was Done

## Result

## Tests / Evidence

## Model / Agent / Runtime

## Artifacts / Code / PRs

## Jira

## Costs / Usage

## Problems / Warnings

## Remaining Work
```

Work detail must prominently show:

```text
HANDOFF
Artifact UID
View Markdown
Download
Copy link
```

Do not force humans to manually fill an empty Add Handoff form after successful automated work.

---

# 10. Correct STNA-87 / Jira Template Fidelity

The previous acceptance ticket did not faithfully use STNA-86.

Do not repeat that.

For historical STNA-87:

```text
preserve ticket
preserve evidence
add correction note if useful
link its canonical ACMS handoff artifact explicitly
```

For future explicitly authorized AI-created Jira tickets:

```text
CLONE STNA-86
```

Do not approximate the template from memory.

Preserve current template structure.

Validate expected sections before moving to `TO START`.

If Jira cloning is unavailable:

```text
fetch STNA-86 current fields/description
create structurally equivalent ticket
compare sections
fail if required sections are absent
```

No ordinary ACMS autonomous ticket creation.

---

# 11. Work / Jira Relationship

ACMS Work and Jira are related, not identical.

Work detail must always show:

```text
ACMS Work UID
ACMS state

Jira key
Jira status
```

Do not collapse them into one status.

Example:

```text
ACMS: RUNNING
Jira: TO START
```

can be valid.

---

# 12. ACMS Work Kanban

Replace the primary flat Work list with an ACMS Kanban view.

This is separate from Jira Kanban.

Columns:

```text
PLANNED
DISPATCHING
RUNNING
HUMAN ATTENTION / BLOCKED
IN REVIEW
COMPLETE / FAILED
```

Keep table/list view as an alternate.

Each card shows:

```text
Work UID
title
Project
Product if any
Jira key + Jira state
Agent
effective model
elapsed time
last activity
cost
artifact count
```

Click Work UID -> Work detail.

Old tests/proofs must be clearly labeled:

```text
TEST
PROOF
ARCHIVED
```

and must not look like current production work.

---

# 13. Project Data Model / UI

Projects are durable context pages.

Every Work Item belongs to a Project.

Projects may exist without Jira.

Expected common case:

```text
1 Jira ticket -> 1 Project
```

but do not enforce strict one-to-one at DB level because Projects can exist without Jira and future Projects may span multiple Jira issues.

Project page shows:

```text
Project name / UID
Product association
repositories
Jira mappings
Sprint mappings
tags
model preference
current work
completed work
artifacts
Inbox items
recent decisions/context
recent agent activity
cost/usage
```

Do not create one Project per Work Item automatically.

---

# 14. Product Data Model / UI

Products remain important.

Product = durable business/customer offering.

Show:

```text
name
business description
status
Projects
repositories
Jira mappings
Sprints
tags
model preference
work counts
cost/usage
Slop Ratios
Slop Ratio history
```

Allow Projects to be associated/disassociated clearly.

Human can correct ACMS-inferred associations.

---

# 15. Repository Associations

Products and Projects may have repositories.

Create durable repository entity if needed.

Fields:

```text
repository_id
owner/org
repo
canonical URL
default branch
Product links
Project links
last measured commit SHA
last measurement time
LOC
ADR counts
```

Avoid duplicate repository rows when one repo belongs to multiple contexts.

---

# 16. Slop Ratio Metrics

Metric name:

```text
Slop Ratio
```

For each Product show two ratios.

## 16.1 True Slop Ratio

```text
True Slop Ratio
=
Lines of Code
/
Human-written ADRs
```

For v1, count an ADR as "human-written" when it contains or links at least one durable human-authored decision / answered question.

Store separately:

```text
human_written_adr_count
human_answer_count
```

This allows later refinement.

## 16.2 Precision Slop Ratio

```text
Precision Slop Ratio
=
Lines of Code
/
Total written ADRs
```

Interpretation:

```text
Too high:
AI may be building too much without enough human direction.

Too low:
humans may be spending too much time discussing/documenting instead of building.
```

Do not hard-code good/bad thresholds yet.

Collect baseline history first.

## LOC rules

Use a language-aware counter.

Exclude:

```text
.git
vendor
node_modules
venv
generated build output
vendored third-party code
```

Document exact exclusions.

## ADR rules

Count accepted/current ADR documents.

Do not count templates or deleted docs.

## Multi-repository Product

```text
Product LOC = sum unique associated repository LOC
```

Do not double-count one repo linked to multiple Projects under one Product.

Apply same unique-source rule to ADR counts.

## Refresh

Default:

```text
hourly
```

Also provide:

```text
Refresh now
```

Store timeseries:

```text
timestamp
LOC
human ADR count
total ADR count
True Slop Ratio
Precision Slop Ratio
commit SHAs
```

Show history chart.

---

# 17. Cost and Electricity - ACMS Becomes Primary Human View

The facility collector already exists in LLM Manager / VM114.

Do not build another Emporia collector.

LLM Manager remains collector/provider.

ACMS becomes the main human cost/usage dashboard.

Authoritative mapping:

```text
Emporia server#1 = PDU MIAM-00151
Emporia server#2 = PDU MIAM-00152

```

Facility:

```text
TOTAL MARION_IA_USA
=
PDU151 + PDU152 + PDU153 + mini split
```

## ACMS ingest

Create a supported internal integration from LLM Manager power API to ACMS.

Do not duplicate raw Emporia credentials into ACMS.

Store normalized interval summaries as needed for history/attribution.

## ACMS Usage / Cost page

Show:

```text
LOCAL TOKENS
CLOUD TOKENS

```

# 18. Home Dashboard

```text
What needs my attention?
```

## Zone 1 - Work Now

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"content": "1|\"\"\"Immutable human-readable work/assignment keys (ACMS-REQ-052; ADR-0010).\n2|\n3|Allocation is a durable, transactional counter — never a row count — so keys\n4|survive deletes and concurrent transactions. Backed by the ``acms_key_counters``\n5|table (portable across SQLite and PostgreSQL; a PostgreSQL sequence would also\n6|satisfy the requirement but the counter table keeps the unit-test path and the\n7|production path identical).\n8|\n9|Format: ``ACMS-WORK-000001`` / ``ACMS-ASG-000001``. Keys are never reused:\n10|the counter only ever increments, even when rows are deleted.\n11|\"\"\"\n12|from __future__ import annotations\n13|\n14|from sqlalchemy import String, text\n15|from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession\n16|from sqlalchemy.orm import Mapped, mapped_column\n17|\n18|from .db import Base\n19|\n20|WORK_PREFIX = \"ACMS-WORK-\"\n21|ASG_PREFIX = \"ACMS-ASG-\"\n22|\n23|\n24|async def _next_counter(db: AsyncSession, name: str) -> int:\n25|    \"\"\"Atomically increment and return the counter for ``name``.\"\"\"\n26|    await db.execute(\n27|        text(\n28|            \"INSERT INTO acms_key_counters (counter_name, counter_value) \"\n29|            \"VALUES (:name, 0) ON CONFLICT (counter_name) DO NOTHING\"\n30|        ),\n31|        {\"name\": name},\n32|    )\n33|    row = await db.execute(\n34|        text(\n35|            \"UPDATE acms_key_counters SET counter_value = counter_value + 1 \"\n36|            \"WHERE counter_name = :name RETURNING counter_value\"\n37|        ),\n38|        {\"name\": name},\n39|    )\n40|    value = row.scalar_one()\n41|    return int(value)\n42|\n43|\n44|async def allocate_work_key(db: AsyncSession) -> str:\n45|    n = await _next_counter(db, \"work_key\")\n46|    return f\"{WORK_PREFIX}{n:06d}\"\n47|\n48|\n49|async def allocate_assignment_key(db: AsyncSession) -> str:\n50|    n = await _next_counter(db, \"assignment_key\")\n51|    return f\"{ASG_PREFIX}{n:06d}\"\n52|\n53|\n54|def extract_work_key_from_title(title: str | None) -> str | None:\n55|    \"\"\"Find an ACMS-WORK-###### key anywhere in a harness session title.\"\"\"\n56|    if not title:\n57|        return None\n58|    import re\n59|\n60|    m = re.search(r\"ACMS-WORK-\\d{6,}\", title)\n61|    return m.group(0) if m else None\n62|\n63|\n64|class KeyCounterRecord(Base):\n65|    \"\"\"Durable allocation counters (keys + event sequence). Row per counter;\n66|    value only ever increments (keys/sequences never reused).\"\"\"\n67|\n68|    __tablename__ = \"acms_key_counters\"\n69|\n70|    counter_name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), primary_key=True)\n71|    counter_value: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(nullable=False)\n72|", "total_lines": 71, "file_size": 2356, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

UNKNOWN stays NULL — never fabricated).\n        model_id=get_settings().worker_model_profile or None,\n    )\n    db.add(rec)\n    await add_event(db, event_type=\"EXECUTION_SESSION_OPENED\", actor_source=\"dispatch_lifecycle\",\n                    agent_id=agent_id, work_key=None,", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"ADR-0012 — worker completion callback (Accepted 2026-09-29).\n2|\n3|Canonical completion architecture:\n4|    Bridge/worker completion callback = PRIMARY immediate completion signal\n5|    ACMS reconciliation sweep          = FALLBACK / repair mechanism\n6|\n7|A callback carries durable semantic data only:\n8|    task/run id, terminal status (SUCCEEDED|FAILED|CANCELLED),\n9|    completed_at, result/handoff reference, usage/cost references when\n10|    available, concise error summary when failed.\n11|It NEVER carries full transcripts / token streams / verbose reasoning.\n12|\n13|Identity binding (fail-closed): the callback must present\n14|    acms_agent_id + execution task id (+ a2a run id when known)\n15|and the recorded task must already belong to that agent. A runtime cannot\n16|close another runtime's task. Unknown task ids are REJECTED with an audit\n17|event; wrong agent identity fails CLOSED with an audit event.\n18|\n19|Idempotency/authority:\n20|    duplicate callback        → harmless (idempotent success, no dup close)\n21|    late callback after close → harmless (idempotent)\n22|    unknown task id           → 404 + audit event\n23|    wrong agent/runtime       → 403 fail-closed + audit event\n24|    lost callback             → reconciliation eventually repairs state\n25|\"\"\"\n26|from __future__ import annotations\n27|\n28|from datetime import datetime, timezone\n29|from typing \"CANCELLED\")\n40|\n41|\n42|class CompletionCallbackError(Exception):\n43|    \"\"\"Machine-readable callback failure (message = stable error code).\"\"\"\n44|\n45|    def __init__(self, code: str, {}\n50|\n51|\n52|class CompletionCallbackRequest(BaseModel):\n53|    \"\"\"Durable semantic payload only — never transcripts or token streams.\"\"\"\n54|\n55|    task_id: str = already_terminal} — both are\n70|    idempotent successes. {"output": "async def _reconcile_execution_results(self, db: AsyncSession, s, now_dt: datetime) -> None:\n        \"\"\"ADR-0012 fallback: close nonterminal ACMS tasks/sessions whose A2A\n        run is terminal on the bridge (lost callback / partition / restart /\n        late callback repair path). Primary signal remains the callback.\n\n        Rate-limited: sweeps at most every ``ACMS_CALLBACK_RECONCILE_SECONDS``\n        (default 300s) so a tick loop never hammers the bridge.\n        \"\"\"\n        interval = max(60, int(getattr(s, \"callback_reconcile_seconds\", 300)))\n        last = self._last_result_reconcile\n        if last is not None and (now_dt - last).total_seconds() < interval:\n            return\n        self._last_result_reconcile = now_dt\n\n        from . import bridge as _bridge_mod\n\n        get_bridge_for_agent = _bridge_mod.get_bridge_for_agent\n        from .work_models import ExecutionTaskRecord\n\n        nonterminal = (await db.execute(\n            select(ExecutionTaskRecord)\n            .where(ExecutionTaskRecord.status == \"RUNNING\")\n            .where(ExecutionTaskRecord.external_task_id.isnot(None))\n            .where(ExecutionTaskRecord.external_task_id.notlike(\"disp-%\"))\n            .order_by(ExecutionTaskRecord.started_at.asc())\n            .limit(20)\n        )).scalars().all()\n        repaired = 0  # empty task batch still falls through to the orphan-session sweep\n        for task in nonterminal:\n            if not task.agent_id:\n                continue  # unassigned task rows are never bridge-reconciled\n            try:\n                bridge = get_bridge_for_agent(task.agent_id)\n                run = bridge._get(f\"/v1/runs/{task.external_task_id}\")\n            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — unreachable runtime: try next sweep\n                continue\n            run_status = (run or {}).get(\"status\") or \"\"\n            terminal_map = {\"succeeded\": \"SUCCEEDED\", \"completed\": \"SUCCEEDED\",\n                            \"failed\": \"FAILED\", \"cancelled\": \"CANCELLED\",\n                            \"canceled\": \"CANCELLED\"}\n            mapped = terminal_map.get(run_status.lower())\n            if not mapped:\n                continue\n            task.status = mapped\n            task.finished_at = now_dt\n            # ADR-0012 §C5: reconciliation repairs SESSION state too — close the\n            # OPEN ExecutionSession correlated to this A2A run (fail-safe: never\n            # reopen or fabricate; only OPEN sessions for the same run id).\n            from .memory_models import ExecutionSessionRecord as _ESR\n\n            sess = (await db.scalars(\n                select(_ESR)\n                .where(_ESR.a2a_task_id == task.external_task_id)\n                .where(_ESR.status == \"OPEN\")\n                .order_by(_ESR.started_at.desc())\n                .limit(1)\n            )).first()\n            session_id = None\n            if sess is not None:\n                sess.status = \"CLOSED\"\n                sess.ended_at = now_dt\n                session_id = sess.session_id\n            repaired += 1\n            await add_event(\n                db, event_type=\"EXECUTION_RECONCILED\", actor_source=\"result_reconciliation\",\n                agent_id=task.agent_id, work_key=None,\n                a2a_task_id=task.external_task_id,\n                summary=(f\"Reconciler closed task {task.task_id[:12]} → {mapped} \"\n                         f\"from bridge run status '{run_status}' (callback lost/late)\"),\n                metadata={\"task_id\": task.task_id, \"a2a_run_id\": task.external_task_id,\n                          \"session_id\": session_id, \"source\": \"adr0012_reconciliation\"},\n            )\n        if repaired:\n            await db.commit()\n\n        # Orphaned-session repair: OPEN sessions whose task is ALREADY terminal\n        # (e.g. task closed by an older deploy, session left OPEN) — close them\n        # from bridge truth as well (ADR-0012 §C5 repairs session state).\n        from .memory_models import ExecutionSessionRecord as _ESR\n\n        open_sessions = (await db.scalars(\n            select(_ESR)\n            .where(_ESR.status == \"OPEN\")\n            .where(_ESR.a2a_task_id.isnot(None))\n            .order_by(_ESR.started_at.asc())\n            .limit(20)\n        )).all()\n        session_repaired = 0\n        for sess in open_sessions:\n            agent = sess.agent_id\n            if not agent:\n                continue\n            try:\n                bridge = get_bridge_for_agent(agent)\n                run = bridge._get(f\"/v1/runs/{sess.a2a_task_id}\")\n            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — unreachable runtime: try next sweep\n                continue\n            run_status = ((run or {}).get(\"status\") or \"\").lower()\n            if run_status in (\"succeeded\", \"completed\", \"failed\", \"cancelled\", \"canceled\"):\n                sess.status = \"CLOSED\"\n                sess.ended_at = now_dt\n                session_repaired += 1\n                await add_event(\n                    db, event_type=\"EXECUTION_RECONCILED\", actor_source=\"result_reconciliation\",\n                    agent_id=agent, work_key=None, a2a_task_id=sess.a2a_task_id,\n                    summary=(f\"Reconciler closed orphaned OPEN session {sess.session_id[:12]} \"\n                             f\"from bridge run status '{run_status}'\"),", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

### [2026-10-01T23:14:28.918699+00:00] assistant

Full picture acquired. Now building Release 2. Design decisions:

- **UID format** `ACMS-WORK-######-YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS` reuses the **same counter** as the existing REQ-052 short key (so `ACMS-WORK-000127` and `ACMS-WORK-000127-20261001_082241` always refer to the same item — session-alignment machinery stays compatible)
- **Backfill**: existing work rows derive seq from their `work_key`; artifacts get a fresh `artifact_key` counter, seq assigned by `created_at ASC, artifact_id ASC`
- **Handoff hook** at both completion authorities (callback primary + reconciler fallback), idempotent, agent-content-never-overwritten

Let me check the `_now()` helpers and response models, then write the code.

### [2026-10-01T23:20:18.906731+00:00] assistant

11/12 — the concurrent test fails on SQLite (single session, aiosqlite can't interleave transactions). That's a test-design issue; the atomic `UPDATE...RETURNING` concurrency guarantee is a PostgreSQL property. The honest fix: verify uniqueness sequentially on SQLite + add true concurrency coverage in the PG integration test. Let me check the PG test file pattern:

---

The existing tests avoid calling `db.close()` because the generator handles it. My `finally: await db.close()` double-closes. Removing all my explicit `db.close()` calls should fix it:

# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

[Subdirectory context discovered: work/llm-manager-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

{"output": "\"\"\"Scoped service authentication (plan §11).\n\nMVP: scoped bearer credentials from a root-owned 0600 file, private network,\naudited, rotatable outside Git. Token file format:\n\n    # service   = <token>\n    svc-acms    = <token>\n    svc-server-manager = <token>\n\nEach caller identity carries an action scope. Documented TDR: temporary\nservice token → future mTLS (plan §11/§19).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport hmac\nimport os\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom functools import lru_cache\n\nfrom fastapi import HTTPException, Request\n\n# Scope names (least-privilege defaults per §10B/§13)\nSCOPES_READ = {\"runtime:read\", \"job:read\", \"route:read\", \"usage:read\", \"health:read\"}\nSCOPES_WRITE = {\"runtime:write\", \"runtime:destroy\", \"job:submit\"}\nALL_SCOPES = SCOPES_READ | SCOPES_WRITE\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ServiceIdentity:\n    service: str\n    scopes: frozenset[str]\n\n    def can(self, scope: str) -> bool:\n        return scope in self.scopes\n\n\ndef _load_identities() -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    \"\"\"Returns {bearer_token: (service, scopes)} from the tokens file.\n\n    File format (0600, outside Git):\n        # service=token:scope1,scope2\n        svc-acms=token1:runtime:read,runtime:write,job:read,job:submit\n        svc-server-manager=token2:ALL\n    \"\"\"\n    path = os.environ.get(\"SERVER_MANAGER_SERVICE_TOKENS_FILE\", \"/etc/llm-manager/secrets/service_tokens\")\n    out: dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]] = {}\n    try:\n        with open(path) as f:\n            for line in f:\n                line = line.strip()\n                if not line or line.startswith(\"#\") or \"=\" not in line:\n                    continue\n                service, rest = line.split(\"=\", 1)\n                token, _, scope_s = rest.partition(\":\")\n                token = token.strip()\n                if not token:\n                    continue\n                if scope_s.strip() in (\"\", \"ALL\"):\n                    scopes = frozenset(ALL_SCOPES)\n                else:\n                    scopes = frozenset(x.strip() for x in scope_s.split(\",\") if x.strip())\n                out[token] = (service.strip(), scopes)\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n    return out\n\n\n@lru_cache(maxsize=1)\ndef _identities_cached(mtime: float) -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    return _load_identities()\n\n\ndef _identities() -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    path = os.environ.get(\"SERVER_MANAGER_SERVICE_TOKENS_FILE\", \"/etc/llm-manager/secrets/service_tokens\")\n    try:\n        mtime = os.stat(path).st_mtime\n    except OSError:\n        mtime = 0\n    return _identities_cached(mtime)\n\n\ndef constant_time_lookup(token: str) -> ServiceIdentity | None:\n    identities = _identities()\n    for tok, (service, scopes) in identities.items():\n        if hmac.compare_digest(tok, token):\n            return ServiceIdentity(service=service, scopes=scopes)\n    return None", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Scoped bearer identity required.\nModel routes + usage read from the EXISTING llmmanager DB (strangler: read\nonly this sprint; writes stay in the proven app).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Request\n\nfrom server_manager.common.auth.service_tokens import require_identity\nfrom server_manager.common.db.session import get_session_factory\n\nrouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/api/v1\", tags=[\"server-manager-v1-llm\"])\n\nAPI_CONTRACT_VERSION = \"1.0.0\"\n\n\n@router.get(\"/model-routes/{route}\")\ndef get_model_route(route: str, request: Request):\n    \"\"\"A model route = a row of model_registry (+ its backends). Read-only.\"\"\"\n    require_identity(request, \"route:read\")\n    Session = get_session_factory(\"llm\")\n    with Session() as session:\n        from sqlalchemy import text\n\n        row = session.execute(\n            text(\"SELECT logical_model_name, engine, backend_url, context_limit, health, routable \"\n                 \"FROM model_registry WHERE logical_model_name = :m\"),\n            {\"m\": route},\n        ).mappings().all()\n        if not row:\n            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f\"no model route {route!r}\")\n        return {\"route\": route, \"api_contract_version\": API_CONTRACT_VERSION,\n                \"backends\": [dict(r) for r in row]}\n\n\n@router.get(\"/usage\")\ndef usage(request: Request, hours: int = 24):\n    \"\"\"Aggregated token usage from LiteLLM_SpendLogs (read-only, §10).\"\"\"\n    require_identity(request, \"usage:read\")\n    if hours < 1 or hours > 24 * 30:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=422, detail=\"hours must be 1..720\")\n    Session = get_session_factory(\"litellm\")\n    with Session() as session:\n        from sqlalchemy import text\n\n        rows = session.execute(text(\n            \"\"\"\n            SELECT model,\n                   count(*)                     AS requests,\n                   COALESCE(sum(prompt_tokens),0)     AS prompt_tokens,\n                   COALESCE(sum(completion_tokens),0) AS completion_tokens\n            FROM \"LiteLLM_SpendLogs\"\n            WHERE \"startTime\" >= now() - make_interval(hours => :h)\n            GROUP BY model\n            ORDER BY requests DESC\n            LIMIT 50\n            \"\"\"\n        ), {\"h\": hours}).mappings().all()\n        return {\"window_hours\": hours, \"api_contract_version\": API_CONTRACT_VERSION,\n                \"models\": [dict(r) for r in rows]}\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------ ORM reads\n# TDR-0008 slice 1: reflective ORM models over hosts/active_hosts/model_registry.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}
